What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hello. Well, what brings me in is that my head just feels really cloudy lately. I'll sit there and stare at a board packet or an email from a vendor, and I just... I can't decide on even the simple things. Things I used to handle fast, without a second thought, I just get stuck on now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel sad. I don't feel much of anything, honestly. Just... stuck, like I said. Not down or crying or anything like that. Just this fog that won't lift.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I'm not nervous or... or on edge. I don't have any kind of jittery feeling. It's really just that fog I mentioned. I just sit there looking at the paperwork and my brain just kind of stalls out.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Well, this low, cloudy feeling... it's been going on for about two weeks now. It started right around the time my husband retired. He's suddenly home all day, and I just noticed this... sharper change in my energy and my confidence right around then.\n\nDoctor: Are you still able to complete your daily tasks and responsibilities despite feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I mean, I'm still getting to work. I haven't missed any days. But I have had to slow down a lot on making decisions. I've been asking my assistant to double-check all the vendor details for me, just to be sure. And I've had to put off a couple of items for the board because... well, I just don't really trust my own judgment right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: No, my sleep hasn't really changed. I'm still getting my usual amount at night. I just feel so drained during the day anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying activities or hobbies that you typically found pleasurable before this cloudy feeling started?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I usually love going to my dance class, or just... walking around looking at the little art galleries. I always cook a big Sunday dinner for my family, too. Those are the things that usually make me feel like myself. But lately, I just haven't wanted to do any of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. I just... I keep thinking I let the residents down. I manage their community, and lately I feel bad because I was speaking too big, you know? Promising things to them before I actually checked the money to see if we could even do it. It's been weighing on my mind a lot. Even though nobody has actually said I ruined anything or complained, I still just feel really guilty about it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean... I can't think of anything right off hand. Usually there's always something, but lately... no. It all just feels like a chore. I don't really look forward to much of anything at the moment.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: I haven't stepped on a scale to check my actual weight, but my eating has definitely been off. Usually, just coffee and a few bites of something will carry me through most of the day now. And then at night... I just pick at some tortillas or sweets, but I don't even really enjoy eating them.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of harming yourself lately?\n\nPatient: Well, I haven't wanted to actually hurt myself. I don't have any plan to do that. But... there have been a few mornings lately where I wake up and just have this scary thought that maybe it would be easier if I just didn't wake up at all. It's just a thought, though. I don't want to actually do anything to myself.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I don't have any plan to do that. But... there have been a few mornings lately where I wake up and just have this scary thought that maybe it would be easier if I just didn't wake up at all. It's just a thought, though. I don't want to actually do anything to myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight since this cloudy feeling started?\n\nPatient: Like I just mentioned, I haven't actually stepped on a scale to know for sure. So as far as I can tell, it's really just my appetite that's off, not necessarily my weight.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Well, it kind of goes both ways. When I'm at home, yes, I move pretty slow. It just feels like my legs are heavy. But then... when I'm at work, like in my association meetings, it's the exact opposite. I'll catch myself constantly tapping my pen or shifting around in my chair. I just feel way too keyed up to sit still.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about how you would hurt yourself if you were to act on those thoughts about not waking up?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. Things I used to handle fast, without a second thought, I just get stuck on now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel sad. I don't feel much of anything, honestly. Just... stuck, like I said. Not down or crying or anything like that. It started right around the time my husband retired. He's suddenly home all day, and I just noticed this... sharper change in my energy and my confidence right around then.\n\nDoctor: Are you still able to complete your daily tasks and responsibilities despite feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I mean, I'm still getting to work. I haven't missed any days. But I have had to slow down a lot on making decisions. I've been asking my assistant to double-check all the vendor details for me, just to be sure. I'm still getting my usual amount at night. I just... I keep thinking I let the residents down. I manage their community, and lately I feel bad because I was speaking too big, you know? Promising things to them before I actually checked the money to see if we could even do it. It's been weighing on my mind a lot. Even though nobody has actually said I ruined anything or complained, I still just feel really guilty about it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really. I don't want to actually do anything to myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight since this cloudy feeling started?\n\nPatient: Like I just mentioned, I haven't actually stepped on a scale to know for sure.
